## Session: Desktop App Scoping — Tauri Conversion + Bundled Compilers (RESEARCH ONLY, PLAN AWAITING APPROVAL) (2026-07-24)

**Scoping/research session only — no `tauri init`, no app code changes, nothing installed.** Investigated whether the app can convert from a web app to a Tauri desktop app, and whether it's feasible to bundle real Java/C#/C++ compilers so Forge's code-review/execution features could run genuine compiled languages locally instead of relying on Pyodide (Python-only) or a server. This is a plan for Joey to review before any conversion work starts, not completed work.

* **Next.js/Tauri compatibility — confirmed this app is a near-best-case candidate.** Tauri requires a static export (`output: 'export'` in `next.config.js`) since it has no Node server behind the webview; no server-based Next.js features (API routes, server actions, middleware) survive static export. Checked the live app: zero `app/api/*` routes exist, and all 9 App Router page files are already `"use client"` with no server components, `"use server"` actions, or middleware found anywhere in `app/`. State is already localStorage-driven. The only real code change needed is adding `generateStaticParams()` to the two dynamic routes (`[tierId]`, `[topicId]`, under both `app/tier/` and `app/forge/`) so static export can pre-render them, plus a config check for `next/image` usage (needs `images.unoptimized: true` if present — not yet checked).
* **Windows prerequisites confirmed**: Rust via rustup (MSVC toolchain, Tauri 2.9.x needs rustc ≥ 1.77), Visual Studio 2022 Build Tools with the "Desktop development with C++" workload + Windows SDK (the heaviest, most likely-to-surprise prerequisite), WebView2 Runtime (already preinstalled on this machine's Windows version), Node 20+. Nothing unusual beyond the VS Build Tools install.
* **Bundled-compiler feasibility scoped for all three target languages, all confirmed freely redistributable**: OpenJDK/Eclipse Temurin (~100 MB download/~290 MB installed — explicitly avoid Oracle's own JDK build, whose OTN license restricts redistribution), .NET SDK (heaviest at ~200–250 MB compressed, since compiling from source needs the full SDK not just the runtime), MinGW-w64/WinLibs g++ (~100–110 MB, smallest and lowest-risk). Combined installer estimate: roughly 500–700 MB compressed / 1–1.5 GB installed if all three ship in the base installer — flagged "download compiler on first use" as an alternative that would cut the base installer to under ~150 MB.
* **Rust-side compiler shell-out pattern scoped, not implemented**: a `#[tauri::command]` writes submitted source to a temp file, shells out to the bundled toolchain binary via `std::process::Command` (app-relative path, not system PATH), captures stdout/stderr/exit code, returns it to the JS frontend via Tauri's IPC. This fully replaces the earlier `child_process`-in-Next.js-API-route idea from prior planning, since Tauri's own Rust backend takes over that role entirely once there's no Node server to run one on. Flagged that sandboxing (timeout, output size caps) needs to be designed in from the start since this executes arbitrary user-submitted code locally.
* **Confirmed zero changes needed for Pyodide or native JS execution** — both already run fully inside the browser/webview engine, and Tauri's webview is a full WebView2/Chromium engine, not a stripped-down runtime.
* **Signing/distribution researched**: unsigned Windows installers trigger a SmartScreen warning (OV certs don't eliminate it, only EV certs do, and EV certs now require an HSM — no longer available as an exportable file, a real cost/complexity jump); unsigned Mac apps get Gatekeeper-quarantined with a misleading "damaged" message (Developer ID cert + notarization is $99/yr, but users can still right-click → Open to bypass). GitHub Releases confirmed as a free, standard host for the installer artifacts either way — for a personal/small-distribution app, shipping unsigned with a clear README note is the practical default rather than paying for signing up front.

**Verdict: feasible for all three languages, with the app itself needing minimal changes for static export.** Proposed build order (not yet started): (1) add `output: 'export'` + `generateStaticParams()`, validate a working static `out/` build before touching Tauri at all; (2) install Rust/MSVC/VS Build Tools, `tauri init` against the validated export; (3) get a bare-bones Tauri shell running the existing app with zero compiler bundling, to validate the wrapper alone; (4) bundle MinGW first (smallest, lowest-risk) and wire up one `run_compiler` command end-to-end for C++ only; (5) repeat for Java then C#, deciding per-language on bundle-vs-download-on-first-use based on the size numbers above; (6) package the installer and decide signing posture.

**This is a plan awaiting Joey's approval — no conversion work has started.** Next step is his go/no-go call on the plan itself, and if approved, which step of the build order to start with.

## Session: Desktop App Step 1 — Next.js Static Export Conversion (COMPLETE) (2026-07-24)

Executed step 1 of the approved Tauri build order: converted the Next.js app to a valid static export, with zero Tauri/Rust/compiler work touched (explicitly out of scope this session, per plan). No ads/monetization considerations apply to this app either way, since it doesn't run any.

* **`next.config.js`**: added `output: "export"`, telling Next.js to produce plain static HTML/CSS/JS files instead of running a server.
* **`next/image` check**: grepped the whole `app/` and `components/` tree — zero usage found, so no `images.unoptimized` change was needed (the scoping session had flagged this as a maybe, not a certainty).
* **`generateStaticParams()` added to *three* dynamic routes, not the two originally scoped** — the kickoff named `[tierId]` and `[topicId]` under `app/tier/` and `app/forge/`, but `app/tier/[tierId]/study/page.js` is a third, separate dynamic route nested under `[tierId]` that the first build attempt caught directly (`next build` refused to proceed without it). All three (plus the two Forge equivalents) now pull their param lists live from `data/knowledge_base.json` at build time — tier IDs for the tier-level routes, and a flattened union of every tier's topic IDs (including all 5 of Expert's language-track topic lists merged, since Expert has no top-level `topics` array) for the topic-level routes. No hardcoded ID lists.
* **Real architectural constraint discovered and worked around**: Next.js does not allow a file marked `"use client"` to also export `generateStaticParams()` — that export has to run in a plain server-side module, but every one of this app's dynamic-route pages was itself a full client component. Fixed by splitting each of the 5 affected route files: the original interactive content moved unchanged into a new sibling `*Client.js` file (`TierPageClient.js`, `StudyPageClient.js`, `TopicHubClient.js`, `ForgeTierClient.js`, `ForgeTopicClient.js`), and `page.js` became a thin server wrapper that exports `generateStaticParams()` and renders the client component. No logic was changed in the move — component bodies are byte-identical to before, just relocated.
* **Second build error, also fixed**: static export additionally requires any component calling `useSearchParams()` to be wrapped in a `<Suspense>` boundary (needed so the exporter has something to pre-render while the URL's query string resolves at load time in a real browser). All 5 of the new client components read `useSearchParams()` (for `?lang=` and `?topic=`), so all 5 server-wrapper `page.js` files now wrap their client component in `<Suspense fallback={null}>`.
* **`next build` succeeded**: 628 static HTML pages generated in `out/` (every tier × topic combination, including all 5 Expert language tracks), zero errors. A handful of "took more than 60 seconds, retrying" lines appeared mid-build under 11-way parallel generation — those are worker-retry log lines, not failures; final count matched 628/628 and the build exited clean.
* **Verified live by serving `out/` locally** (`npx serve out`, a plain static file server, not `next start` — confirming the build has zero runtime Next.js server dependency) and checking through the Browser pane:
  - Dashboard: loads, reads localStorage-backed progress correctly (rank, XP, tiers earned).
  - Study pages across multiple tiers: Novice (`what_is_a_computer`), Legend (`cap_theorem`), and Expert's `?lang=python` track-scoped view — all rendered correctly, including deep-linking straight to a specific topic via `?topic=`.
  - Glossary highlighting/popover/link-resolution: confirmed on Legend's `cap_theorem` page — the base "CAP theorem" term correctly still opens its original definition and its "Read full page →" link correctly resolved to `/tier/journeyman/study?topic=nosql_database_types` (its original tier), proving cross-tier glossary routing survives the export unchanged. (One false alarm during manual testing: repeated clicks toggle the popover open/closed, and an odd/even mismatch briefly looked like a broken popover — resolved by checking DOM state directly rather than assuming click count.)
  - Forge: opened an Expert/Python topic (`py_list_comprehensions`), typed real code into the answer box, clicked Run, and got genuine Pyodide-executed output (`[144, 176, 120, 190]`) — confirming Python still runs for real inside the static-served webview-equivalent, not a stub. Same check for native JS on an Expert/JavaScript topic (`js_var_let_const`) — `console.log` output came back correctly (`hello from static export 4`).
  - Progression page: correctly reflected the topic-read count accumulated during this test session, confirming localStorage read/write round-trips correctly against the static build.
  - Stopped the local `serve` process and its Node processes after verification.

**Step 1 of the Tauri build order is complete and confirmed working end-to-end — the app is now a valid, fully-static Next.js export with no server dependency, matching Tauri's `frontendDist` requirement.** The only source changes were: `next.config.js` (`output: "export"`), 5 new `*Client.js` files (content moved, not rewritten), and 5 rewritten `page.js` wrappers (now server components exporting `generateStaticParams()` and a `Suspense` boundary). No content, styling, or app logic changed.

**Step 2 is next, per the approved build order**: install Rust (via rustup, MSVC toolchain) + the Tauri CLI + Visual Studio 2022 Build Tools ("Desktop development with C++" workload + Windows SDK) on this Windows machine, then run `tauri init` against this now-validated `out/` export. No session has started step 2 yet; needs Joey's go-ahead to proceed, same as every build-order step in this plan.
